</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Blue; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic">Roman Catholic</a> (53.4%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Darkorchid; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christian</a> (25.7%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Gold; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Animism" title="Animism">Animism</a> (3.2%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:MediumSeaGreen; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Muslim" class="mw-redirect" title="Muslim">Muslim</a> (10.4%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Pink; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other (6.0%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:black; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;None / No Answer (1.4%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p>Religions practised in Gabon include <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> (<a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholicism" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholicism">Roman Catholicism</a> and <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestantism</a>), <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a>, and traditional indigenous religious beliefs.<sup id="cite_ref-report_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-report-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Some people practice elements of both <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> and indigenous religious beliefs.<sup id="cite_ref-report_39-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-report-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Approximately 79% of the population (53% Catholic) practice one of the denominations of <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a>; 10% practice <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> (mainly <a href="/wiki/Sunni" class="mw-redirect" title="Sunni">Sunni</a>); the remainder practice other religions.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Gabon" title="Health in Gabon">Health in Gabon</a></div>
<p>A private hospital was established in 1913 in <a href="/wiki/Lambar%C3%A9n%C3%A9" title="Lambaréné">Lambaréné</a> by <a href="/wiki/Albert_Schweitzer" title="Albert Schweitzer">Albert Schweitzer</a>. By 1985 there were 28 hospitals, 87 medical centers, and 312 infirmaries and dispensaries. As of 2004<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, there were an estimated 29 physicians per 100,000 people, and "approximately 90% of the population had access to health care services".
</p><p>In 2000, 70% of the population had access to "safe drinking water" and 21% had "adequate sanitation". A government health program treats such diseases as <a href="/wiki/Leprosy" title="Leprosy">leprosy</a>, <a href="/wiki/African_trypanosomiasis" title="African trypanosomiasis">sleeping sickness</a>, <a href="/wiki/Malaria" title="Malaria">malaria</a>, <a href="/wiki/Filariasis" title="Filariasis">filariasis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Intestinal_worms" class="mw-redirect" title="Intestinal worms">intestinal worms</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Tuberculosis" title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a>. Rates for immunization of children under the age of 1 were 97% for tuberculosis and 65% for <a href="/wiki/Polio" title="Polio">polio</a>. Immunization rates for <a href="/wiki/DPT_vaccine" title="DPT vaccine">DPT</a> and <a href="/wiki/Measles" title="Measles">measles</a> were 37% and 56% respectively. Gabon has a domestic supply of pharmaceuticals from a factory in Libreville.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> has decreased from 5.8 in 1960 to 4.2 children per mother during childbearing years in 2000. 10% of all births were "low birth weight". The <a href="/wiki/Maternal_mortality_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Maternal mortality rate">maternal mortality rate</a> was 520 per 100,000 live births as of 1998. In 2005, the <a href="/wiki/Infant_mortality_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="Infant mortality rate">infant mortality rate</a> was 55.35 per 1,000 live births and life expectancy was 55.02 years. As of 2002, the overall mortality rate was estimated at 17.6 per 1,000 inhabitants.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS" title="HIV/AIDS">HIV/AIDS</a> <a href="/wiki/Prevalence" title="Prevalence">prevalence</a> is estimated to be 5.2% of the adult population (ages 15–49).<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> As of 2009<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, approximately 46,000 people were living with HIV/AIDS.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> There were an estimated 2,400 deaths from AIDS in 2009 – down from 3,000 deaths in 2003.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Gabon" title="Education in Gabon">Education in Gabon</a></div>
<p>Its education system is regulated by two ministries: the Ministry of Education, in charge of pre-kindergarten through the last high school grade, and the Ministry of Higher Education and Innovative Technologies, in charge of universities, higher education, and professional schools.
</p><p>Education is compulsory for children ages 6 to 16 under the Education Act. Some children in Gabon start their school lives by attending nurseries or "Crèche", then kindergarten known as "Jardins d'Enfants". At age 6, they are enrolled in primary school, "École Primaire" which is made up of 6 grades. The next level is "École Secondaire", which is made up of 7 grades. The planned graduation age is 19 years old. Those who graduate can apply for admission at institutions of higher learning, including engineering schools or business schools. As of 2012, the literacy rate of a population ages 15 and above was 82%.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The government has used oil revenue for school construction, paying teachers' salaries, and promoting education, including in rural areas. Maintenance of school structures, and teachers' salaries, has been declining. In 2002 the gross primary enrollment rate was 132%, and in 2000 the net primary enrollment rate was 78%. Gross and net enrollment ratios are based on the number of students formally registered in primary school. As of 2001, 69% of children who started primary school were "likely" to reach grade 5. Problems in the education system include "poor management and planning, lack of oversight, poorly qualified teachers", and "overcrowded classrooms".<sup id="cite_ref-ilab_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ilab-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Gabon" title="Culture of Gabon">Culture of Gabon</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:TsogoMask.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/TsogoMask.jpg/220px-TsogoMask.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="330" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/TsogoMask.jpg/330px-TsogoMask.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/TsogoMask.jpg/440px-TsogoMask.jpg 2x" data-file-width="853" data-file-height="1280" /></a><figcaption>A Gabonese mask</figcaption></figure>
<p>A country with a primarily <a href="/wiki/Oral_tradition" title="Oral tradition">oral tradition</a> until the spread of literacy in the 21st century, it has <a href="/wiki/Folklore" title="Folklore">folklore</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mythology" class="mw-redirect" title="Mythology">mythology</a>. "Raconteurs" are working to keep traditions alive such as the mvett among the Fangs and the ingwala among the Nzebis.
</p><p>It features internationally celebrated masks such as the n'goltang (Fang) and the reliquary figures of the <a href="/wiki/Kota_people_(Gabon)" title="Kota people (Gabon)">Kota</a>. Each group has its own set of masks used. They are used in ceremonies such as marriage, birth and funerals. Traditionalists work with "rare local woods and other precious materials".
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Gabon" title="Music of Gabon">Music of Gabon</a></div>
<p>It has an array of folk styles. Imported rock and hip hop from the US and UK are in Gabon, as are <a href="/wiki/Cuban_Rumba" class="mw-redirect" title="Cuban Rumba">rumba</a>, <a href="/wiki/Makossa" title="Makossa">makossa</a> and <a href="/wiki/Soukous" title="Soukous">soukous</a>. Some folk instruments include the obala, the <a href="/wiki/Ngombi" title="Ngombi">ngombi</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Balafon" title="Balafon">balafon</a> and drums.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Media_of_Gabon" class="mw-redirect" title="Media of Gabon">Media of Gabon</a></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+link+.ambox{margin-top:-1px}html body.mediawiki .mw-parser-output .ambox.mbox-small-left{margin:4px 1em 4px .ambox-delete{border-left:10px solid #b32424}.mw-parser-output .ambox-content{border-left:10px solid #f28500}.mw-parser-output .ambox-style{border-left:10px solid #fc3}.mw-parser-output .ambox-move{border-left:10px solid #9932cc}.mw-parser-output .ambox-protection{border-left:10px solid #a2a9b1}.mw-parser-output .ambox .mbox-text{border:none;padding:0.25em .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-Unreferenced_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">August 2022</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Radio-Diffusion Télévision Gabonaise (RTG) which is owned and operated by the government broadcasts in <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a> and indigenous languages. Color television broadcasts have been introduced in some cities. In 1981, a commercial radio station, Africa No. 1, began operations. It has participation from the French and Gabonese governments and private European media.
</p><p>In 2004, the government operated 2 radio stations and another 7 were privately owned. There were 2 government television stations and 4 privately owned. In 2003, there were an estimated 488 radios and 308 television sets for every 1,000 people. About 11.5 of every 1,000 people were cable subscribers. In 2003, there were 22.4 personal computers for every 1,000 people and 26 of every 1,000 people had access to the <a href="/wiki/Internet" title="Internet">Internet</a>. The national press service is the Gabonese Press Agency which publishes a daily paper, <i>Gabon-Matin</i> (circulation 18,000 as of 2002).
</p><p><i><a href="/wiki/L%27Union" title="L&#39;Union">L'Union</a></i> in Libreville, the government-controlled daily newspaper, had an average daily circulation of 40,000 in 2002. The weekly <i>Gabon d'Aujourdhui</i> is published by the Ministry of Communications. There are about 9 privately owned periodicals which are either independent or affiliated with political parties. These publish in certain numbers that have been delayed by financial constraints. The constitution of Gabon provides for free speech and a free press, and the government supports these rights. Some periodicals actively criticize the government and foreign publications are available.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Gabonese_cuisine" title="Gabonese cuisine">Gabonese cuisine</a></div>
<p>Gabonese cuisine is influenced by French cuisine, and <a href="/wiki/Staple_foods" class="mw-redirect" title="Staple foods">staple foods</a> are available.<sup id="cite_ref-Foster_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Foster-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_Gabon" class="mw-redirect" title="Sport in Gabon">Sport in Gabon</a></div> 
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Gabon_national_football_team" title="Gabon national football team">Gabon national football team</a> has represented the nation since 1962.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Gabon_national_under-23_football_team" title="Gabon national under-23 football team">Under-23 football team</a> won the <a href="/wiki/2011_CAF_U-23_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="2011 CAF U-23 Championship">2011 CAF U-23 Championship</a> and qualified for the <a href="/wiki/Football_at_the_2012_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_tournament" title="Football at the 2012 Summer Olympics – Men&#39;s tournament">2012 London Olympics</a>. Gabon were joint hosts, along with <a href="/wiki/Equatorial_Guinea" title="Equatorial Guinea">Equatorial Guinea</a>, of the <a href="/wiki/2012_Africa_Cup_of_Nations" title="2012 Africa Cup of Nations">2012 Africa Cup of Nations</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> and the sole hosts of the competition's <a href="/wiki/2017_Africa_Cup_of_Nations" title="2017 Africa Cup of Nations">2017 tournament</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Gabon_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Gabon national basketball team">Gabon national basketball team</a>, nicknamed <i>Les Panthères</i>,<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> finished 8th at the <a href="/wiki/AfroBasket_2015" title="AfroBasket 2015">AfroBasket 2015</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Gabon_at_the_Olympics" title="Gabon at the Olympics">Gabon</a> has competed at most Summer Olympics since 1972. Its Olympic medallist <a href="/wiki/Anthony_Obame" title="Anthony Obame">Anthony Obame</a> won a silver medal in <a href="/wiki/Taekwondo" title="Taekwondo">taekwondo</a> at the <a href="/wiki/2012_Summer_Olympics" title="2012 Summer Olympics">2012 Olympics</a> held in London.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gabon has <a href="/wiki/Recreational_fishing" title="Recreational fishing">recreational fishing</a> and is considered the "best place in the world" to catch <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_tarpon" title="Atlantic tarpon">Atlantic tarpon</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Wildlife_of_Gabon">Wildlife of Gabon</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Wildlife of Gabon">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Wildlife_of_Gabon" title="Wildlife of Gabon">Wildlife of Gabon</a></div>
<p>Gabon has a large number of protected animal and plant species. The country's biodiversity is one of the most varied on the planet.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Fauna_of_Gabon">Fauna of Gabon</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Fauna of Gabon">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Gabon is home of 604 species of birds, 98 species of amphibians, between 95 and 160 species of reptiles and 198 different species of mammals.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> In Gabon there are rare species, such as the Gabon <a href="/wiki/Pangolin" title="Pangolin">pangolin</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Grey-necked_rockfowl" title="Grey-necked rockfowl">grey-necked rockfowl</a>, or endemics, such as the Gabon <a href="/wiki/Guenon" title="Guenon">guenon</a>.
</p><p>The country is one of the most varied and important fauna reserves in Africa:<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> it is an important refuge for <a href="/wiki/Chimpanzee" title="Chimpanzee">chimpanzees</a> (whose number, in 2003, was estimated between 27,000 and 64,000)<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> and <a href="/wiki/Gorilla" title="Gorilla">gorillas</a> (35,000 recorded in 1983).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The "Gorilla and Chimpanzee Study Station" inside the <a href="/wiki/Lop%C3%A9_National_Park" title="Lopé National Park">Lopé National Park</a><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> is dedicated to their study.
</p><p>It is also home to more than half the population of <a href="/wiki/Loxodonta_cyclotis" class="mw-redirect" title="Loxodonta cyclotis">African forest elephants</a>,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> mostly in <a href="/wiki/Mink%C3%A9b%C3%A9_National_Park" title="Minkébé National Park">Minkébé National Park</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Gabon's national animal is the <a href="/wiki/Black_panther" title="Black panther">black panther</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora_of_Gabon">Flora of Gabon</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Flora of Gabon">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>More than 10,000 species of plants, and 400 species of trees form the flora of Gabon. Gabon's <a href="/wiki/Rainforest" title="Rainforest">rainforest</a> is considered the densest and most virgin in Africa. However, the country's enormous population growth is causing heavy deforestation that threatens this valuable ecosystem. Likewise, poaching endangers wildlife. Gabon's national flower is <a href="/wiki/Delonix_regia" title="Delonix regia">Delonix Regia</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabon&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
